# Definitions of Done

> **TL;DR:** Setup is complete only when all three upstream installs coexist, their versions are evidenced and the Design Library docs can be read. Future component quality remains unproven until its own evidence exists.

## Owner instructions

**The single home for every owner instruction** (owner, 2026-11-07 20:13: *"Define a DoD. Every time I give instructions, it should go in the DoD immediately."*). Each instruction becomes a row here in the same turn it arrives, before any build, and every packet is checked against these rows. Quotes are verbatim (the owner's spelling), times +05:30, mined from both main-chat logs on 2026-11-07. **Done** means delivered and awaiting the owner's judgement; nothing is approved unless the [ledger](work-plan.md#approval-ledger) says so. Workflow rules (roles, cadence, delegation) live in AGENTS.md and the work plan.

**Open gaps (2026-11-07 20:42):** Q24 clipping is partial; Q25 loading delivered (F13c); Q26 scrollbars is partial (F14 delivered, F14b next after typography); Q35 typography is the next packet (F16); Q28 exhaustive components is starting with Button (F15.1); Q28's message is cut off after "We need to". The size framework (Q28, Q21) is written from the owner's words in [Button](#button-f151) for confirmation; no other size message exists in the logs.

| Q171 | 11-17 10:19 | "Why do we keep getting dropdown wrong. How hard is it to get the padding correct in all versions of the dropdown button." (screenshots: the AlignUI "Dropdown" example's "Account" trigger, more space after the chevron than before the label) | (1) **Cause, measured:** the Q165 edge rule (`--edge-ink-end` in `controls.css`) sets a trailing icon's ink inset equal to its top and bottom ink inset. That is right for a square glyph and wrong for a chevron, whose ink is flat (about 11×6 px in a 16 px icon), so the trailing gap is (height − 6) / 2 and grows with the control's height while the label's start gap stays at the padding. Probe of AlignUI's "Account" MenuButton (size m, 48 px): label ink 9 px from the start, chevron ink 21.7 px from the end. Computed for core MenuButton (padding 8, chevron 16): xs 24 px 9 vs 9, s 36 px 9 vs 15, default 44 px 9 vs 19, m 48 px 9 vs 21, l 56 px 9 vs 25, xl 64 px 9 vs 29, xxl 80 px 9 vs 37; Select is the same rule from its field padding (13 vs 15–21). It looked right only at the small size it was checked at; nothing measured the other sizes. (2) **Fix at the source:** one rule for every dropdown-like trigger (MenuButton, Select, NumberField's preset segment, a Button with a trailing chevron): the chevron's ink sits as far from the trailing edge as the label's ink sits from the leading edge, at every size, emphasis, density and template theme; the top/bottom-matching rule stays only for square glyphs at an edge (a leading icon, icon-only buttons, Clear). (3) **Kept true:** a probe measures label-start and chevron-end ink gaps for every dropdown-like trigger at every size and emphasis in core and each template, light and dark, and fails when they differ by more than 1 px | Probe table before and after; the AlignUI and coss Dropdown, Select and the library MenuButton and Select pages at every size | Q165, Q166, Q167 | Delivered for review (2026-11-17 10:35), `dl-integrate` commit `31bcb68` on `integrate/word-into-brand-map` (pushed), live on 8770. **Fix at the source:** `pickers.css` (MenuButton) and `controls.css` (Select): the chevron's end margin is the leading inset − the chevron's ink side whitespace + its ink shift, where the leading inset is the start padding token (`c.menu-button.padding-inline`; `c.field.padding-inline`, `-sm`, `-lg` per size) before words, an avatar or a flag, or the leading glyph's own ink inset before an icon (Icon lends that glyph's measured ink height to the row as `--lead-ink-h`). Select's chevron left its square cell. Square glyphs (leading icon, icon-only, Clear, NumberField scrub and common-values segment) keep the top/bottom rule. No template overrides, prop or token changes. NumberField's preset segment is icon-only, so it has no label to match; no Button uses a trailing chevron. **Kept true:** `npm run probe:dropdown-inset` (`tools/dropdown-inset-probe.mjs`, listed in AGENTS.md). **Before:** 156 of 168 triggers failed, e.g. library Select xxl 18 vs 34 px, MenuButton default 10 vs 19, alignui Account (m) 9 vs 21, coss Options (s) 9 vs 15. **After:** 0 of 296 fail across library MenuButton (new Sizes story, xs–xxl, default, quiet, primary with an icon) and Select (xs–xxl, words and icon), coss and alignui (Dropdown and Select now show every size of their scale), word-figma; light, dark, default and compact; worst diff −1 px, the first letter's own side bearing. Slack has no dropdown trigger with words. Checks: tokens:check, typecheck, check:project, check:clipping, agent:index:check, library build pass. | Q170 | 11-17 09:45 | "Set up another worker to figure out why the color page is laggy?" (http://127.0.0.1:8770/#/colour) | (1) The cause of the lag on the library's Colour page is found with evidence: a performance trace of the interactions that feel slow (load, dragging a colour picker or slider, switching presets or brand examples, scrolling), naming the long tasks, the components that re-render and the work in them (colour maths, token regeneration, style recalculation, layout), in the dev build and a production build, never guessed (performance skill: no invented numbers). (2) The fix goes in the owning source (core component, behaviour or the page's data flow), not a page-level patch, and the same interactions are measured again before and after. (3) Nothing else moves | The Colour page at `http://127.0.0.1:8770/#/colour`, its slow interactions before and after, with the trace numbers | Q35, F15 | Done for review (2026-11-17), `dl-integrate` commit `31bcb68` on `integrate/word-into-brand-map` (pushed), live on 8770. **Cause** (headless Edge, CDP traces and CPU profiles, dev and production builds): every move of a colour picker re-runs the brand draft and recolours the whole page, and five pieces of avoidable work rode on top of that. (a) `primitiveIn` (the library's token lookups) copied the whole theme token map on every call, thousands of times per move: 3,015 ms of a 9,746 ms script profile for a 31-move hue drag (dev). (b) Core Icon's ink-box measure (Q165) was a new ref on every render and retried `getBBox` on icons inside closed popups, forcing a full-page style flush on every move: 2,845–3,100 ms of the same profile. (c) About 500 contrast cells each read `getComputedStyle` and rendered twice per move (10,552 reads over 20 moves). (d) Core ScrollArea's content watcher measured synchronously after each React commit (1,504 ms once (a)–(c) were fixed). (e) Scrolling: ScrollArea set its thumb variables on the region that wraps the whole page, so every wheel step restyled all ~5,800 elements. **Fix**, each at its source:<br>• `primitiveIn` caches per context (`apps/library/src/data/tokens.ts`).<br>• Brand `apply()` writes only the variables and sheet text that changed (`data/brand.ts`).<br>• Contrast cells re-measure in a transition instead of on every move (`inspectors/inspectors.tsx`).<br>• Core Icon keeps one ref per glyph and measures unmeasured glyphs after layout, in a ResizeObserver (`fundamentals/Icon.tsx`).<br>• Core ScrollArea sets its thumb variables on the track, measures content changes once per frame and observes each child once (`behaviour/scroll.ts`).<br>• Core CHANGELOG has an Unreleased entry.<br>**Before → after, production build** (same probe, same machine, 1440×900):<br>• Area drag, 62 moves: median move-to-frame 162 → 90 ms, p90 218 → 119 ms, long tasks 10,502 → 5,785 ms.<br>• Hue drag: median 151 → 96 ms, long tasks 9,213 → 6,297 ms.<br>• Scroll, 30 wheel steps: long tasks 28 → 2, 2,230 → 240 ms; elements restyled 175,356 → 25,059.<br>• Preset 311 → 130 ms; brand example 268 → 117 ms; arrangement 83 ms → no long task.<br>• Load long tasks 1,150 → 776 ms.<br>**Dev:** area drag median 245 → 160 ms, hue 253 → 134 ms; drag script profile 9,746 → 3,156 ms.<br>**Behaviour unchanged:** the same 72 contrast ratios update after a drag, the same icons carry ink variables, no page errors. No ResizeObserver loop errors in either build, so that warning is ruled out as a cause. Typecheck, `check:project` (0 errors) and the library build pass.<br>**What remains:** a colour drag still restyles the whole page on every move. That costs about 62 ms in production even for a custom property nothing reads, because Chrome re-matches all ~2,750 rules against all ~5,800 elements (selector stats show no single rule dominates). The measured next step is not shipped and needs the owner's call: `content-visibility: auto` on off-screen page sections cut that cost to 27 ms. It adds paint containment, which could clip focus rings and shadows at section edges (owner rule), and it changes every page's layout, so it also needs the regression sweep |

| Q152 | 11-12 16:42 | "- Articulate and capture how this library is supposed to work, technically.<br>- Use that to define a strict framework that define how ai agents should do the pressure-loop test<br>- Use a scorecard and rating system to independently check if the library stays true to how it is supposed to work. | Q149 | 11-12 12:13 | "Our slate library is getting bloated.<br>- Clean up and archive old, incoherent and completed point in time records.<br>- Address the following report on how we have adopted to harden the library with the custom template based approach.<br>## How the library gets better by building templates on it<br>It works like this: build a real product as a template using only the library. Each place where the library doesn't fit is a finding. The fix goes to the lowest level that can hold it: a seam that already exists, then a part inside the template, then a reviewed change to the core library. The library's checks then stop the same mistake from happening again.<br>The rules for this exist, but they're spread across three documents and don't fully agree. Nothing describes the loop end to end.<br>### 1. What a template is<br>The library has tiers: tokens → fundamentals → compounds → layouts → templates, plus content. Each tier is built only from the tiers below it. A template is a folder with: a manifest that lists its layouts and every custom part it adds (a part that isn't listed doesn't exist); a content schema and content written as pure JSON; its own tokens, which may only point at semantic roles; migration steps. The rule that drives the loop is in `foundation.md`: **customizing never forks the core.**<br>### 2. How a limitation shows up and where it gets fixed<br>When something doesn't look or behave as needed, you take the first step on the `seam ladder` that works: 1. An existing prop or variant. 2. A slot (passing another part in). 3. A context attribute on a parent, such as `data-density`. 4. The theme or an allowed theme setting. 5. A **custom part inside the template**. 6. A **core change**: a new variant or slot, reviewed on its own. Needing step 5 or step 6 is the finding. It shows exactly where the library falls short.<br>### 3. What a template may and may not do<br>The customization rules are in `foundation.md`. A template's custom part: may use only the core's public exports, semantic tokens and its own `t.{template}.*` tokens; styles only its own markup, inside its own namespace and CSS layer; may never restyle a core component, use `!important`, or patch, wrap-and-replace or copy a core component. The rule is **"ask the core for a variant instead"**; must be documented like a core part: doc comment, typed props, stories, WCAG AA in light, dark and more-contrast modes, 44 px targets, focus ring and reduced motion.<br>### 4. How a fix reaches the core<br>- **Promotion** (`foundation.md`): when two templates need the same custom part, it becomes a candidate for the core. It moves only through a core change with its own review, and then the templates switch to the core part.<br>- **Fixes found while building the library's own templates are made generic in the core.** The Design tool audit is the clearest example (`F15.D1`). It produced new slots on EditorLayout, a PropertyRow compound, `hideLabel` on Select and the `data-density` context. Card now owns the rule for its picture's corners (`F15.D-x`). Each was "never a per-template override or fork".<br>- **The Conference template** is the worked example. Its own parts (Countdown, SponsorWall, SponsorsLayout) were built with the core unchanged except for one layer name. That shows the template layer can hold real product needs.<br>### 5. How the rules are enforced<br>- `check:content`, `check:views`, `check:layers`, `check:clipping`, `check:materials`, `tokens:check`, and the style and spacing lints.<br>- `npx design-library-check` on consumer projects.<br>- A review loop (`AGENTS.md`): an evaluator who didn't build the screen scores it, and each failure is fixed where it originates (theme → layout → props → custom part → content), never on the rendered page.<br>### 6. DesignUp's side of the loop<br>DesignUp is an outside site using the library, so it can't change the core itself. When the library has no seam for something, DesignUp: adds a small, scoped, commented override in the site; logs it on `Library requests` with the need, the exact limitation, the override's file and lines, the proposed API and the removal step. LR1, the two-column footer, is an example.<br>## What's missing<br>1. **No single procedure for the loop.** Nothing describes the whole cycle: find a limitation → log it → decide core or template → fix it → check it → remove the override.<br>2. **Two rules contradict each other.** foundation.md fixes the library's own templates' gaps in the core right away ("every fix generic in core"). The consumer `AGENTS.md rule 6` and seam ladder step 6 allow a core change "only when two or more products need it". Nothing says which rule applies when.<br>3. **No log format.** There's no shared format for recording a limitation (ID, evidence, the ladder step reached, proposed API, status). DesignUp's library-requests page is the only working version.<br>4. **No triage rules.** Nothing says how to decide whether a fix belongs in the core, in a custom part, or in a temporary override plus a library request.<br>5. **No definition of done for a fix.** A fix should count as done only when: the core change ships with stories and checks; the template's override or custom part is deleted; the request is marked shipped with the version.<br>6. **No feedback into the checks.** Nothing requires a repeated miss to become a new check or a new row in "rules agents miss".<br>7. **Nobody owns or reviews the requests.** No one is named to review them and there's no set review rhythm.<br>**Recommended next step:** a worker writes one short rulebook, "Template pressure-test loop", in foundation.md … It would close the seven gaps above and link to DesignUp's library-requests page as the consumer-side log.<br>Set up a new worker and use Gemini 4 carbon for this work." | **Q149.1** The Slate site (`design-library/docs`) is trimmed: old, incoherent and completed point-in-time records (finished packet logs, superseded plans, stale trackers, old release briefs, resolved decision packets) move to an archive section. | Q65 | 11-08 20:34 | "Develop and entire framework and philosophy around transitions and animations. What I want is a functional transition that conveys how context and content move from one state to the next. I want to rely heavily on morphing of content and content seamlessly and continuously wherever it makes sense. The idea is that if a context/content is present in both state, whether the same or an evolved one, the content should ideally morph seamlessly instead of disappearing and reappearing again. This can become a complex and expensive operation when done and applied everywhere. So, we ca be smart and optimal about it. Many state transition can only transition the wrapper and fake the content within. Various transition of a dialog into a success/error msg etc can all morph into smotth seamless transition by using the same wrapper so that nothing is flickering and breaks seamlessness. Any addition of shadoes, transition of border color, bg color etc should all be animated in value rather than a sudden jump and change in value. We don't want this at the cost of making the experience feel laggy. So we do most of these at 200ms time unless specifically extended where needed and in special places, we might want to completely not have this transition, like while changing the numeric value of an number input using arrow key, any lag in reflecting the value will give a laggy fill. We have to clearly identify wherre 0 latency is expected and not have any transition in such places. Use Opus 5.5 extra high thinking and detailed of this framework first. Critique it and improve it. Use the impreved version to audit and prepare a report of the recommended improvements and implementation in the design library. ### Button (F15.1)

Size framework, written from Q28 and Q21 for the owner to confirm: size is **per instance** in seven steps (`xs` 24, `s` 36, `normal` 44, `m` 48, `l` 56, `xl` 64, `xxl` 80 px high), each a token set (height, padding, gap, icon, type); the theme's **space option** changes the space around and between components, never a component's size; the **shape option** changes radii at every size; `normal` and up keep at least a 44 px target, `xs` and `s` are dense sizes under the written exception (WCAG 2.5.8 minimum 24 px).

| ID | Done when (observable) | Evidence / Try it | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B.1 | Seven sizes, xs to xxl, side by side for text, icon + text and icon-only | [Button](http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/button) "Sizes and types" | Done (F15.1) |
| B.2 | Types: icon button (named, with its name as tooltip), dropdown button (MenuButton at the same sizes), segmented (SegmentedControl, no second component), button group (fundamental) | [ButtonGroup](http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/buttongroup), MenuButton in the size matrix, Button "Segmented button" | Done (F15.1b, about 21:14): split button = Button + icon-only MenuButton in an attached ButtonGroup (no new component); MenuButton gains emphasis, icon-only and busy (spinner inside, same width, won't open); SegmentedControl on the seven-step scale (`s` default, old `sm`/`md` = `xs`/`s`). Open: the ring variant for MenuButton |
| B.3 | In progress for every type, two variants: spinner inside, and an arc travelling around the button's border; no width change; busy announced; arc ≥ 3:1 | Button "In progress: spinner inside", "…on the border", "Try both" | Done (F15.1) |
| B.4 | Danger emphasis; unavailable with a reason (focusable, says why) | Button "Emphasis", "Unavailable" | Done (F15.1) |
| B.5 | Every size under the four space and four shape options keeps its height; only radii and surrounding space change | Space and shape pages' whole-library preview, then the Button page | Done by construction (size tokens alias size.*, never space.*); not yet inspected in all eight options |
| B.6 | Q34 holds: no one-sided borders, no redundant variant, Switch still the binary setting | Review | Self-checked; toggle buttons not built (owner decision open) |

Open: segments alternate the accent's strong and soft tones because no categorical colour set exists (same gap as category badges); the soft tone is under 3:1 against the surface, so the legend text carries the meaning. Drag-a-whole-segment, add/remove parts: later |
| S.8 Gradient stop editor | New compound [GradientEditor](http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/gradienteditor): bar painted with the gradient (mirrors in RTL); stops as outlined swatch pins (`role=slider`, "30 %, #3366ff"), selected stop ringed; drag to move, click the bar to add with the interpolated colour, drag off or Delete to remove (two always remain, said when refused); selected stop's colour opens the platform picker (Enter on a stop too), position box, previous/next stop for crowded stops; Add stop (widest gap), Remove stop (unavailable with reason at two), Flip, Distribute evenly; verified via CDP | GradientEditor page, four examples | Done (F15.10d). Later: alpha and checkerboard (the platform picker has no alpha), eyedropper, interpolation modes and colour spaces, midpoints |

## Future product Definition of Done

These rows are targets, not achieved results.

| ID | Promise / capability | Evidence needed | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PD1 | P1/U1: component reuse does not produce divergent behavior. | Source-derived component purpose/props/tokens/stories; representative flows; no redundant variants or tier violations. | Not implemented |
| PD2 | P1/U1/U2: accessibility works across themes, states and viewports. | Keyboard and screen-reader observations, accessibility checks, contrast measurements, 320 px layout and focus/scrolling checks. | Not implemented |
| PD3 | P1/U2: tokens and validated view data drive composition. | Deterministic generated token output, alias/ownership/contrast tests, data-validation failures and no view-specific style escape. | Not implemented |
| PD4 | P2/U3: decisions and quality claims remain grounded. | Linked assumptions and decisions, updated as-built notes, tests/observations beside each completed row. | Intent documented; product evidence absent |
| PD5 | P1/P2: waits, notifications and failures remain coherent. | Local pending/confirmed/failed state examples; input preserved, recovery available, auto-dismiss default verified, motion/announcements checked. | Not implemented |
| PD6 | P2: performance claims are measurable. | Representative workload, chosen metric/budget, reproducible baseline and before/after comparison. | Not measured |
| PD7 | P3/U4/U7: customization spans declared web UI families without replacing fundamentals. | Proposed first matrix: three UI families, two distinct brands, light/dark/more-contrast; zero component forks or per-view style escapes, and unchanged control behavior. Coverage gaps explicitly recorded. | Proposed target; not implemented |
| PD8 | P4/U5/U6: agents can make reliable contract-driven changes. | Proposed first corpus: six declared tasks (composition and theme adaptation in each of three UI families), all yielding schema-valid proposals within at most one repair round; baseline comparison of time and correction effort in a fixed environment before any "easiest" claim. | Proposed target; no agent baseline |
| PD9 | P2/P4/U6: invalid customization is recoverable and explainable. | All invalid fixture proposals rejected before replacing working output; machine-readable path/reason/recovery, affected-consumer diff and last working configuration preserved. | Proposed target; not implemented |
| PD10 | P1/P3/U8: add-ons are isolated and removable. | Enable on one/selected/all views and disable per subtree/theme; zero fundamental forks/private selectors, unchanged content/actions/focus in unaffected scopes, clean mount/update/dispose. Core works with zero effect-engine/asset requests when disabled. | Proposed; not implemented |
| PD11 | P1/P3/U9: font family/provider swaps need no component edits. | Change only validated theme/provider data; inspect required scripts/weights, text wrapping, blocked/offline/system fallback and metrics. Initial font transfer at most 100 KB compressed for declared script/weights, font CLS at most 0.02 and total CLS at most 0.1 on reference profiles; additional scripts have explicit budgets. | Proposed; no font implementation or measurements |
| PD12 | P1/P3/U10: at least three icon sets retain semantic coverage. | First-flow inventory mapped in Material Symbols, Fluent and Font Awesome Free; 100% names have approved meaning/size/style/RTL mapping or explicit acceptance-blocking gaps with reviewed fallbacks. Same ONE Icon fundamental; initial used-icon data at most 20 KB compressed, zero external icon requests or unreferenced geometry. | Proposed; no provider mappings built |
| PD13 | P2/U11: licence/provenance manifest is complete. | 100% selected fonts/icons/add-on assets have immutable origin/version, licence/use-rights, copyright/NOTICE/credit and redistribution/modification/brand restrictions; generated/distributed output retains applicable notices. No forbidden FFL/Remix packaging or kube.io assets; effect release gates resolved before distribution. | Proposed; research caveats documented, no distribution |
| PD14 | P1/P2/P3/U8: effects retain contrast and performance. | Effects on/off in light/dark/contrast, reduced transparency/motion, forced colours, unsupported browser/WebGL/context loss: every text role at least 4.5:1 and non-text/focus at least 3:1 on worst-case composited surfaces. Proposed p75 LCP at most 2.5 s, INP at most 200 ms, total CLS at most 0.1; at 60 Hz, p95 scrolling frame time at most 16.7 ms and no idle/offscreen animation loop. Same-machine effects-on/off comparison with noise allowance; otherwise limit scope or disable. | Proposed; no effect/performance baseline |

These initial thresholds are reviewable working defaults, not measurements or proof of absolute universality. The owner can revise the matrix/corpus before implementation. No project design-system or agent benchmark tests have been run on this draft.

| PD16 | P1/P3/U12/U13: specimens are faithful across contexts. | `specimen-renderer-fidelity`, `specimen-context-matrix`, `specimen-host-accessibility`: actual product renderer/token/story digests, no docs overrides, all declared context cells explicit, keyboard/portals and first product-host comparison verified. | Proposed; no specimen runner |
| PD17 | P2/U13: a broken candidate cannot remove the approved diagnostic reference. | `frame-specimen-isolation`: inject candidate token/import/render/theme/protocol errors; approved frame/reference remain usable with path/reason/recovery. Plain HTML stays readable when both renderer asset sets are blocked. A browser-wide hang requires external recovery, not an asserted iframe guarantee. | Proposed; no failure-injection evidence |
| PD18 | P2/U3/U13: known-good promotion is explicit and recoverable. | `known-good-promotion-and-recovery`: exact artifact digest plus dated owner approval/context scope; mixed/stale/unapproved artifact rejected; atomic pointer switch and prior artifact retained; first tiny consumer's seed approval recorded. | Proposed; no known-good product artifact |

These are named future checks, not implemented files or tests run on unapproved designs. [Architecture and bootstrap](brief.md#the-library-presents-itself) define the ordinary consumer, stable frame and emergency diagnostic exception.

### Foundation slices (show me the UX sooner)

| ID | Observable result | Evidence | Status |

| S2.2 | A person can see every colour, type, space, radius, elevation and motion decision in light, dark and more contrast (U1, U3, U12). | Library app Foundations pages at `http://127.0.0.1:8766/`; contrast ratios measured live in each context cell. | Built; owner judgement pending |

| F11.1 | Every page opens at its top, and the old page fades out completely before the new one fades in, within 200 ms of motion (owner F11, O52). | Measured in the dev server: about 120 ms to fully out, then render, then about 90 ms in; never two pages at once; focus on the new title; reduced motion swaps at once. | Built; owner judgement pending |
| F12.1 | A theme picks its space (compact, normal, loose, extra loose) and shape (sharp, rounded, soft, super soft) in one config, and the defaults reproduce today exactly (owner F12, O53-O55). | `npm run tokens:check` runs `tools/space-shape.mjs --check` (config, staleness, every invariant in all eight options); 1,140 of 1,140 resolved values unchanged at normal + rounded. | Built; owner judgement pending |
| F12.2 | A person can compare the four options of each side by side, read who each is for, and preview the whole library in any of them (U4, U12). | Space and size and Shape pages at `http://127.0.0.1:8766/#/space` and `#/shape`; 44 px targets unchanged and no sideways scroll at about 320 px in extra loose + super soft (browser check). | Built; owner judgement pending |
| F13a.1 | Anyone can tell where a new floating thing goes, and nothing stacks by a raw number (owner F13, O56). | `npm run check:layers` passes (10 layers, 6 uses, 0 raw z-index); the Layers page lists each layer with its components, read from source. | Built; owner judgement pending |

**Against the promise:** Stage 0 and this draft make the project ready to reason, execute and document. P1/P3/P4 are not yet fulfilled as a usable adaptable component library or an AI-friendly framework. Core proof comes first; source extraction, provider swapping/coverage, add-on isolation/removal, licence completeness and effects-on contrast/performance then need their proposed evidence. Source review is not integration validation or licence clearance: UI Lighting's scoped fit, Liquid Glass's release licence/asset gate, restricted font/icon distribution and all budgets remain future checks. Component implementation waits for a separately approved bounded stage.

Self-presentation and its known-good recovery are now specified, not built. Rapid review is the current operating agreement, with two distinct documentation deliveries; product approval and the longer three-round outcome remain unproven. No frame promotion or execution-mode escalation follows from these documentation checks.

## Stage: DesignUp improvements

> **TL;DR:** The Definition of Done for the work that comes out of the first real adoption (owner Q104, Q105, Q107, Q108): six promises, twelve things a person can do, and observable rows per release (0.2.0-beta.2, 0.3.0-beta.1, 0.4.0-beta.1 "Data-driven sites", later) plus rows for the stage as a whole. The plan behind it is the [improvement plan](archive/improvement-plan.md). The coverage matrix below maps every item in both DesignUp notes, the plan's own findings and the Beta known issues to a row here or to a recorded out-of-scope decision, with **0 unmapped**. The stage's final evidence is DesignUp's own re-run (BS.8). **Q110 adds** promise DUP7, user-can U13–U14, [Migration readiness](#migration-readiness-q110) rows BM.1–BM.6 (0.4.0-beta.2) and B5.1–B5.8 (0.5.0-beta.1), stage rows BS.9–BS.10, and a second matrix for DesignUp's migration-readiness note: 120 items, 100 to rows, 20 out of scope with reasons, **0 unmapped**. **Q111 re-checks** the note's final form: rows BM.7–BM.8 and B5.9, and the matrix grows to 129 items, 108 to rows, 21 out of scope, **0 unmapped**.

**Owner, 2026-11-10 02:08 (verbatim, [Q108](#owner-instructions)):** *"Create a proper DoD and make sure everything is covered from the notes from the DesignUp site."*

**Sources:** the DesignUp beta report (`docs/research/design-library-beta-report.md` in that project) and its data-driven requirement (`docs/research/design-library-data-driven-requirement.md`), both read-only; [Improvement plan](archive/improvement-plan.md) Parts 1–3; [Handoff readiness](archive/handoff-readiness.md#beta-known-issues). Results are recorded beside each row by whoever does the work; the "State" column starts at Planned, and rows already built say so.

### Promises

| # | Promise | Done when | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DUP1 | A brand-led site stays inside the library: its brand needs are met through public seams, not custom parts that redraw core | DesignUp's expressive pages reach a library share of at least half on the re-run, with no core-token override outside the allow-list | BS.8; the project check's `--share` and `core-token` counts |
| DUP2 | Defaults are safe for any brand: text passes AA and motion stops when the person asks | The bright-brand set passes with the guard on; no page motion under reduce | B3.7, B2.3, B4.10 |
| DUP3 | A project enforces the rules itself, by running one command | `npx design-library-check` runs in a project and its findings match a hand audit | B3.20, B3.21, B4.12 |
| DUP4 | An agent gets it right from the guide: names that exist, core parts first | The names check passes; the next proof's share and workaround counts drop | B2.2, B3.27, B3.1, B3.2, BS.5 |
| DUP5 | A site can be strictly data-driven (D1–D8) | AT1–AT6 pass on the reference site | B4.14 |
| DUP6 | Upgrading never breaks a site, and each release says how to adopt it | The upgrade proof on a DesignUp copy shows no change; each release ships a brief | BS.2, BS.3, BS.4 |
| DUP7 | A site moves onto the library completely, keeping only its brand art as named template parts on public extension points (Q110) | DesignUp reports 43 or more of its 51 custom items on core or tokens and the rest inside its six named parts, with no other custom component code | BS.9; BM.1–BM.6, B5.1–B5.8 |
| DUP8 | Pictures and illustrations belong to every scheme: a site's art looks right in light, dark and more contrast, page-wide or in one region, and drawn art fits its space and moves only as the motion rules allow (Q112) | The context-matrix stories of `Image` and `Illustration` pass with no flash or shift on a switch; `check:svg` and the content check pass on every template | BM.9–BM.11, B5.10–B5.14 |

### What a person can do

| # | A person can |
| --- | --- |
| U1 | Install both packages on the npm the guide names, behind a package proxy, first time |
| U2 | Set the page frame, link look, canvas colour, tracking, fonts, header, footer and scheme from the theme, without overriding core |
| U3 | Build common site needs (link buttons, tinted bands, uneven columns, media openings, lead text, crops and treatments) from core props and slots |
| U4 | Pick any brand colour and get AA text |
| U5 | Turn on reduced motion and have every page change, scroll and effect respect it, with no site code |
| U6 | Search a list as they type, and send a form whose fields save together |
| U7 | Follow in-page links, "on this page" lists and letter jumps under hash or history routing |
| U8 | Run one command that lists every rule break with its file, line and fix |
| U9 | Find the core part for a need before writing a custom one |
| U10 | Start an empty template of their own |
| U11 | Add a page, reorder sections, change words or add a collection item by editing JSON alone |
| U12 | Upgrade by following one brief, with nothing breaking |
| U13 | Move between sibling pages (years, versions, items of one kind) without losing their place, and go back to the top of the page they're on from its link |
| U14 | Put brand shapes over or around a core picture, reveal a paired picture in the site's own way, and keep a series of openings the same height, without a custom frame or opening component |
| U15 | Give any picture, photo or drawing, a light and a dark (and a more-contrast) version, in code or JSON, that switches with the scheme of the page or of one region, with nothing flashing or jumping (Q112) |
| U16 | Place a drawn illustration that fits whatever space it's in, takes its colours from the theme in every scheme, and moves only as the motion rules allow, still under reduced motion (Q112) |
| U17 | Use their brand's own icons: a whole set, or just a few that replace the same icons everywhere components and layouts show them, the rest staying as they were (Q113) |

### Migration readiness (Q110)

**Source:** DesignUp's `docs/research/library-migration-readiness.md` (read-only), planned in [Improvement plan, Part 4](archive/improvement-plan.md#part-4-moving-a-site-completely-onto-the-library-q110). Two releases: 0.4.0-beta.2 (the must, the guide answers, part reuse, the direct-jump proof, and, added for Q111, settable top type steps) and 0.5.0-beta.1 (brand media on extension points, and display-size section titles), per ID21. ### Against the promise

Built so far: B2.1–B2.7 (install matrix and guide fixes; reduced-motion swap, theme settings, link decoration, base colour, tracking). Everything else is planned. The promise is furthest away on DUP5 (data-driven sites: nothing of 0.4 is built) and on DUP1, which only DesignUp's re-run can prove (BS.8). The riskiest assumptions to settle first are IA13 (one page schema for templates and sites) and IA9 (shared breakpoints in CSS); both have a cheap test in the plan. Every row that depends on DesignUp's team (BS.8) names them: we never write in that project.
